6Definitions

For the purposes of interpreting and applying this Policy, and in addition to the definitions contained in Law 1581 of 2012, Single Regulatory Decree 1074 of 2015 and any rules amending, supplementing or replacing them, the following definitions are adopted:

Do no harm
An approach according to which the collection, use, analysis, circulation and disclosure of information must be planned and carried out so as to prevent revictimization, discrimination, stigmatization or the creation of new risks to the life, integrity, security, privacy and dignity of data subjects.
Anonymization
The process by which personal data are transformed in such a way that the data subject cannot be identified and re-identification is not reasonably possible. Once irreversibly anonymized, the information ceases to constitute personal data.
Authorization
Prior, express and informed consent given by the data subject for the processing of their personal data, except in the cases provided for by law. Where sensitive data are concerned, authorization must be explicit and the data subject must be informed that providing such data is optional.
Privacy notice
A verbal, written or electronic communication by which the Data Controller informs the data subject of the existence of the Personal Data Processing Policy, how to access it, the purposes of processing and the rights available to them.
Database
An organized set of personal data subject to processing, whether contained in physical, electronic or digital media.
Successor in interest
A natural or legal person who, by operation of law or inheritance, succeeds the data subject in the exercise of their rights and is entitled to act on the terms established by law.
Confidentiality
The duty to ensure that personal data are known, consulted, used or disclosed only by authorized persons and for permitted purposes, throughout the entire life cycle of the information, including after the end of the relationship of anyone who took part in its processing.
Enquiry
A request submitted by the data subject or an entitled person in order to know the personal information held in the databases or files of the Data Controller or Data Processor.
Contractor
A natural or legal person engaged under a contractual relationship to provide services, carry out activities or develop products related to the operation of Caribe Afirmativo or the implementation of its projects, and who may act as a Data Processor when processing personal data on behalf of the Corporation.
Personal data
Any information linked to or that may be associated with one or more identified or identifiable natural persons.
Private data
Information that, by its intimate or confidential nature, is relevant only to the data subject.
Public data
Data classified as such by the Political Constitution or by law, as well as data that are not semi-private, private or sensitive in nature. Public data include, among others, data on a person's civil status, profession or occupation and their status as a merchant or public official, on the terms established by law.
Semi-private data
Information that is neither intimate, confidential nor public in nature and whose knowledge or disclosure may be of interest not only to the data subject but also to a particular sector or group of people.
Sensitive data
Information affecting the data subject's privacy or whose improper use may give rise to discrimination, such as information revealing racial or ethnic origin, political orientation, religious or philosophical convictions, membership of trade unions, social organizations, human rights organizations or groups that promote the interests of political parties or safeguard the rights and guarantees of opposition political parties, as well as data relating to health, sex life and biometric data. For the purposes of this Policy, reinforced protection shall also be given to data relating to sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, sex characteristics, health status, migration status, international protection needs and experiences of violence, persecution or discrimination.
Biometric data
Personal data obtained from physical, physiological or behavioural characteristics that allow a person to be uniquely identified or authenticated, such as facial images used for recognition, fingerprints, the iris, the voice or a biometric signature. Such data shall be regarded as sensitive when they are intended to identify a person unambiguously.
Geolocation or location data
Information that makes it possible to determine or infer a person's geographical location, movements, routes, journeys or frequented places. Its processing shall be subject to reinforced security measures where it may reveal sensitive information or create risks for the data subject.
Personal data of children and adolescents
Information linked to or that may be associated with a person under eighteen (18) years of age. Its processing must respect their best interests, their fundamental rights and their evolving capacities, ensure that their views are heard where appropriate, and comply with the special conditions provided for by law.
Dissociation
A procedure by which personal data are separated from the elements that directly identify the data subject, in order to reduce the risks associated with their processing.
Data Processor
A natural or legal person, public or private, who, alone or jointly with others, processes personal data on behalf of the Data Controller and in accordance with its instructions.
Gender expression
The external manifestation of a person's gender identity, which may be evidenced, among other things, through their appearance, behaviour, clothing, manner of speaking, name or pronouns.
Habeas data
The fundamental right of every person to know, update, rectify and, where appropriate, delete the information collected about them in databases or files, as well as to control its use, circulation and processing and to withdraw the authorization given in the cases permitted by law.
Gender identity
The internal and individual experience of gender as each person feels and recognizes it, which may or may not correspond to the sex assigned at birth.
Images and audiovisual records
Photographs, audio recordings, videos or any other audiovisual record that makes it possible to identify, directly or indirectly, a natural person.
Information security incident affecting personal data
A confirmed or suspected event that compromises or may compromise the confidentiality, integrity or availability of personal data and that may result in their loss, destruction, alteration, consultation, access, use, circulation or unauthorized or fraudulent disclosure.
Interoperability
The capacity of two or more platforms, information systems or technological components to exchange, consult or use information in a coordinated manner, under conditions that guarantee the security, confidentiality and protection of personal data.
Human mobility
The set of movements of people within or outside a territory, temporarily or permanently, voluntarily or by force, including, among other situations, internal or international migration, forced displacement, refuge, asylum, return and migratory transit.
Sexual orientation
A person's capacity to feel emotional, affective or sexual attraction towards other people, as well as to establish intimate or sexual relationships with them.
Participating entity or organization
A social, community, humanitarian, human mobility or human rights organization that takes part in the implementation of the projects, programmes or initiatives of Caribe Afirmativo and that may act as a Data Controller or Data Processor, depending on the decisions it takes and the activities it carries out in relation to personal data.
LGBTIQ+ person
A person who identifies as lesbian, gay, bisexual, trans, intersex, queer or within other sexual orientations, gender identities or expressions and diverse sex characteristics. The use of this acronym in this Policy is inclusive and does not limit the recognition of other forms of identification or self-recognition.
Person in a situation of human mobility
A person who moves or has moved within their country or across an international border, regardless of the causes, duration or composition of the movement or their migration status.
Person with international protection needs
A person who is outside their country of origin or habitual residence and requires protection because of risks to their life, liberty, security or integrity associated, among other circumstances, with persecution, violence, conflict, human rights violations or discrimination on the grounds of their sexual orientation, gender identity or expression or sex characteristics. This includes stateless persons or persons at risk of statelessness, refugees and/or asylum seekers, among other categories.
Supplier
A natural or legal person that supplies goods or provides services to Caribe Afirmativo. Where, by reason of that relationship, it carries out operations on personal data on behalf of the Corporation, it shall have the status of Data Processor and must comply with the obligations set out in the law, in contracts and in this Policy.
Project
For the purposes of this Policy, the project “Building Bridges, Weaving Paths: Promoting Actions for the Enforcement of Rights and the International Protection of LGBTIQ+ People in Situations of Human Mobility in Ibero-America”, as well as the other institutional projects, programmes, strategies or initiatives in which, in accordance with the purposes notified and authorized, the personal data covered by this Policy are processed.
Complaint
A request submitted by the data subject or an entitled person in order to obtain the correction, updating or deletion of their personal data; the withdrawal of authorization, where appropriate; or the review of an alleged breach of personal data protection rules.
Data Controller
A natural or legal person, public or private, who, alone or jointly with others, decides on the database or the processing of personal data. For the purposes of this Policy, Caribe Afirmativo shall act as Data Controller, without prejudice to cases in which another organization may hold that status in accordance with the decisions it takes regarding the information.
Area responsible for personal data protection
The unit in charge of coordinating compliance with this Policy, receiving and handling data subjects' enquiries and complaints, and promoting the implementation of legal, administrative, human and technological measures to protect information. For the purposes of this Policy, this function shall be carried out by the Legal Area of Caribe Afirmativo.
Pseudonymization
Processing by which personal data can no longer be attributed to a data subject without the use of additional information, provided that such information is kept separately and is subject to technical and organizational measures preventing its attribution by unauthorized persons.
Data subject
A natural person whose personal data are processed.
Transfer
An operation by which the Data Controller or Data Processor sends personal data to a recipient, located inside or outside Colombia, who receives the information and acts as Data Controller by deciding on its purposes and means.
Transmission
Processing of personal data that involves their communication, inside or outside Colombia, so that a Data Processor may carry out operations on the information on behalf of and under the instructions of the Data Controller.
Processing
Any operation or set of operations carried out on personal data, such as collection, recording, organization, classification, storage, retention, consultation, use, analysis, updating, rectification, circulation, transmission, transfer, anonymization, pseudonymization, archiving, restriction, deletion or any other form of handling of information.
Authorized user
A natural person who, by reason of their duties, contractual obligations or participation in a project, holds credentials and express permissions to access a platform, information system, file or database. Their access must be limited to the information strictly necessary to carry out the activities assigned to them.

7Principles applicable to the processing of personal data

Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller, and the participating organizations acting as Data Processors shall apply, in a consistent and comprehensive manner, the principles established in article 4 of Law 1581 of 2012. These principles shall guide all operations of collection, recording, organization, storage, consultation, use, updating, circulation, transmission, transfer, retention, anonymization and deletion of personal data carried out in the development of the project “Building Bridges, Weaving Paths: Promoting Actions for the Enforcement of Rights and the International Protection of LGBTIQ+ People in Situations of Human Mobility in Ibero-America”.

Their application shall cover any information system, digital platform, application, form, database, physical or electronic file, technology service or documentary tool that is used, implemented, replaced, updated or integrated for the implementation of the project. Accordingly, the following principles shall be observed:

Legality in data processing

The processing of personal data is a regulated activity and must be carried out in accordance with the Political Constitution, Law 1581 of 2012, Decree 1074 of 2015 and any other applicable national or international provisions. The Corporation and the organizations acting as Data Processors must carry out processing within the competences, instructions, purposes and conditions established by law and by the agreements governing their participation in the project.

Purpose

Processing must serve legitimate, specific, explicit purposes notified in advance to the data subject. Personal data may be processed only in order to carry out the project's activities; to guide, assist and support LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility; to manage and follow up on cases; to facilitate coordination among participating organizations; to protect and promote their rights; to generate duly anonymized statistical information; to comply with legal or contractual obligations; and to pursue the other purposes expressly set out in this Policy and in the corresponding authorization. Data may not be used for purposes incompatible with those that justified their collection.

Freedom

Processing may be carried out only with the prior, express and informed authorization of the data subject, unless there is a legal or judicial mandate or one of the exceptions provided for by law applies. Personal data may not be obtained, consulted, used, circulated or disclosed without authorization or legal grounds. Authorization may be obtained in writing, verbally or through unequivocal conduct that reasonably allows the conclusion that it was given; silence may under no circumstances be understood as authorization. Where sensitive data are collected, the data subject shall be informed that they are not obliged to authorize their processing, and the data to be processed and the corresponding purposes shall be expressly stated.

Truthfulness or quality

Information subject to processing must be truthful, complete, accurate, up to date, verifiable and understandable. The Corporation and the Data Processor organizations shall adopt reasonable measures to verify and maintain data quality. The processing of partial, incomplete, fragmented or outdated information, or information that may be misleading, is prohibited.

Transparency

Data subjects shall be guaranteed the right to obtain from the Corporation, as Data Controller, or from the organizations acting as Data Processors, at any time and without undue restrictions, information about the existence of personal data concerning them. They may also request information about the purposes of processing, the use made of their data and, where appropriate, the organizations or third parties to whom the data have been disclosed.

Restricted access and circulation

Processing is subject to the limits arising from the nature of personal data, the Political Constitution and applicable law. Data may be processed only by persons authorized by the data subject or empowered by law. Access shall be limited to staff, contractors, participating organizations, technology providers and other Data Processors who need to know the information in order to carry out previously defined and authorized functions. Except in the case of public information, personal data may not be made available on the internet or through other mass dissemination or communication media, unless technical mechanisms exist to control access and restrict knowledge of the data to data subjects or to duly authorized third parties.

Security

Caribe Afirmativo and the organizations acting as Data Processors shall implement the technical, human, physical, administrative and organizational measures that are necessary and reasonable to protect personal data against tampering, loss, destruction, consultation, use, modification, disclosure or unauthorized or fraudulent access. These measures must take into account the nature of the information, the conditions of processing, the technologies used and the particular risks associated with handling sensitive data of LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility.

Confidentiality

All persons involved in the processing of personal data that are not public in nature are obliged to keep the information confidential, including after the end of their employment, contractual, professional, institutional or cooperation relationship with the Corporation or with any of the participating organizations. Data may be supplied or disclosed only where this corresponds to the development of authorized activities, to the purposes notified to the data subject or to the cases permitted by law.

Without prejudice to the statutory principles set out above, the Data Controller and the Data Processor organizations shall observe the following complementary criteria:

Necessity and limitation of collection

Only data that are adequate, relevant and strictly necessary to fulfil the notified purposes shall be collected and processed. Requesting, using or retaining excessive information, or information unrelated to the project's objectives or to the relevant institutional activity, shall be avoided.

Time limitation

Data shall be retained for the period that is reasonable and necessary to fulfil the purposes that justified their processing and to meet applicable legal, contractual, administrative, accounting, documentary or legal defence obligations. Once those purposes have been fulfilled and the corresponding periods have expired, the information shall be deleted, anonymized or subjected to mechanisms preventing the identification of data subjects, unless there is a legal duty of retention.

Demonstrated accountability

Caribe Afirmativo and the Data Processor organizations shall adopt appropriate, effective and verifiable measures to guarantee and demonstrate compliance with the personal data protection regime. To that end, they shall implement internal procedures, access controls, records management mechanisms, risk assessments, training, security protocols, incident management and agreements or contracts defining the obligations of those involved in processing.

Privacy by design

The protection of personal data shall be incorporated from the planning, selection, procurement, design, development, configuration, implementation, integration, updating and operation of the processes, information systems, platforms, applications, databases and other technological or documentary tools used in the project.

Privacy by default

The initial configuration of the tools and procedures used must limit processing to the data strictly necessary for each purpose. Access to, retention of and circulation of the information shall be restricted, by default, to authorized persons and for the time strictly required to fulfil the notified purpose.

Differential, gender and do-no-harm approach

Processing shall recognize the particular circumstances of LGBTIQ+ people, migrants, applicants for international protection, refugees and stateless persons or persons at risk of statelessness, and other people in situations of vulnerability. Information relating to sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, health, migration status, ethnic origin, experiences of violence or other sensitive circumstances shall be treated with particular confidentiality and may not be used to generate discrimination, stigmatization, persecution or revictimization, or to harm the dignity, security or integrity of data subjects.

8Processing and purposes of the databases

Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller, shall collect, record, organize, store, retain, consult, update, use, circulate, transmit, transfer, anonymize and, where applicable, delete the personal data necessary for the implementation of the project “Building Bridges, Weaving Paths: Promoting Actions for the Enforcement of Rights and the International Protection of LGBTIQ+ People in Situations of Human Mobility in Ibero-America”.

Processing may be carried out through information systems, digital platforms, applications, forms, databases, storage services, physical or electronic files and any other technological or documentary tool that is used, replaced, updated or integrated during the implementation of the project. Accordingly, this Policy is not limited to any particular platform or technological solution.

The collection and management of information may involve not-for-profit organizations located in different countries, linked to the development of the project and dedicated to the assistance, support and protection of LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility. These organizations shall act as Data Processors when they process personal data on behalf of and following the instructions of Caribe Afirmativo, without prejudice to compliance with the legislation applicable in the country where they carry out their activities.

Data Processor organizations may process personal data only for the purposes defined by the Corporation. They must also apply appropriate security and confidentiality measures and may not use the information for their own purposes or for purposes that are incompatible with or different from those authorized. These obligations are incorporated into the contracts, agreements, cooperation arrangements or other legal instruments governing their participation in the project.

Where the development of the project involves the international transmission or transfer of personal data, the Corporation shall verify in advance compliance with the requirements established in Law 1581 of 2012, Decree 1074 of 2015 and other applicable provisions. It shall likewise establish sufficient safeguards through contracts, agreements, data protection clauses or other legal instruments defining the scope of processing, the authorized purposes, the security measures, the responsibilities of the parties and the mechanisms for guaranteeing data subjects' rights. Personal data shall be processed in accordance with the following purposes:

Data subjectsPurposes of processing
LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility, migrants, refugees, returnees, displaced persons, persons with international protection needs, stateless persons, asylum seekers and other participants or beneficiaries of the project To identify and profile the people who request or receive guidance, support or assistance; to record their contact and location details where necessary; to identify their needs, conditions of vulnerability and risk factors; to manage, document and follow up on cases; to provide legal, psychosocial, humanitarian, migration-related or institutional guidance; to facilitate access to services, programmes, referral pathways and protection mechanisms; to make referrals to competent organizations or authorities where authorization or legal grounds exist; to coordinate services among participating organizations; to prevent situations of violence, discrimination, persecution, exploitation, revictimization or secondary victimization; to contact the person in order to continue providing support; to record and monitor the migration routes or journeys reported or travelled by the person, including through location or geolocation data, where this functionality is enabled and prior, express and informed authorization has been given, in order to understand the conditions of the journey, identify points of interest, warn of possible risk situations, especially on irregular transit routes, and help improve the person's guidance, experience and safety during their journey; to evaluate the services provided; to prepare internal reports and to carry out the other activities directly related to the project's objectives.
People whose sensitive data are necessary for the provision of services or support To process, where strictly necessary, information relating to sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, sex characteristics, health status, disability, ethnic or racial origin, migration status, experiences of violence, international protection needs, statelessness or risk of statelessness, refuge and/or asylum, among other sensitive circumstances; to identify particular risks and needs; to apply differential, gender, intersectional and do-no-harm approaches; to determine appropriate referral pathways; to prevent discrimination and revictimization; and to adopt special measures to protect the person's life, integrity, liberty, dignity and security. Providing these data is optional, unless a legal obligation applies, and explicit authorization shall be required where appropriate.
Family members, companions, carers, representatives, trusted persons and emergency contacts To verify family, care, representation or accompaniment ties; to establish contact in emergency situations; to support case management and follow-up; to coordinate assistance and protection measures; to facilitate family reunification or the restoration of contacts where appropriate; to evidence the corresponding representation or authorization and to maintain communication with the persons authorized by data subjects.
People referred to other organizations, institutions or authorities To manage referrals in order to facilitate access to legal, psychosocial, humanitarian, migration-related, health, education, employment, accommodation or protection services; to verify the availability and continuity of services; to follow up on the referral; to avoid duplication of assistance and to coordinate comprehensive responses. Only the data that are adequate, relevant and necessary for the purpose of the referral shall be disclosed.
Natural or legal persons taking part in surveys, interviews, focus groups, research, assessments and profiling activities To carry out research, studies, assessments, evaluations and needs identification exercises; to understand the circumstances of LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility; to evaluate the scope, relevance and impact of the project; to formulate recommendations; to design intervention strategies and to generate knowledge for the protection and enforcement of rights. Wherever possible, results shall be presented in anonymized or aggregated form.
People who provide testimonies, accounts, photographs, audio recordings, videos or other content To document the project's experiences, results, lessons learned and activities; to produce educational, informational, research or awareness-raising materials; to develop communication and advocacy campaigns; and to disseminate the project's results. Publication of a person's image, voice, testimony, name or any information that makes them identifiable shall require specific, prior and informed authorization defining the media, purposes, territorial scope and conditions of use. Authorization to receive support does not imply authorization for the public dissemination of information.
People attending workshops, sessions, meetings, training and other project activities To manage registration and attendance; to organize the logistics and security of activities; to send invitations, reminders, materials and certificates; to address accessibility needs or reasonable accommodations; to document the conduct of activities; to evaluate their quality and impact; to respond to queries and to maintain communication about activities related to the project, where authorization exists.
Representatives, workers, volunteers and collaborators of participating organizations acting as Data Processors To identify the persons authorized to take part in the project; to manage agreements and cooperation relationships; to assign profiles, credentials, roles and access levels for the tools used; to coordinate activities; to issue processing instructions; to record training; to manage confidentiality commitments; to verify compliance with security protocols; to maintain traceability of the operations carried out; to manage security incidents and to monitor compliance with the obligations assumed by each organization.
Workers, contractors, consultants, volunteers, interns and other members of the Caribe Afirmativo team involved in the project To manage their engagement and participation; to assign roles, profiles and access permissions; to coordinate and supervise activities; to administer fees, payments, social security and contractual or employment obligations; to carry out training and evaluation processes; to manage the Occupational Health and Safety Management System where applicable; to require compliance with confidentiality and security duties; to document the activities carried out and to meet legal, administrative, accounting and audit obligations.
Applicants for jobs, contracts, consultancies, internships or volunteer positions To receive, record and assess CVs; to verify education, experience and references; to conduct interviews and other selection stages; to contact candidates; to take decisions on their engagement and to retain the information for the period authorized or necessary to meet legal obligations and any claims.
Suppliers of goods or services and their representatives, employees or contact persons To manage selection, contracting, performance and contract termination processes; to verify legal, tax, financial, technical and social security requirements; to administer payments, invoicing and accounting records; to supervise compliance with obligations; to control access to premises, systems or information where applicable; to carry out evaluations; to respond to audits, claims and requests from authorities; and to retain supporting documentation for the operations carried out.
Technology service providers To manage the provision of hosting, storage, support, maintenance, security, communications, licensing, backup, recovery and operation services for the tools used; to administer users and access controls; to maintain technical records and traceability; to detect, prevent and respond to failures, security incidents, unauthorized access or fraudulent use. Where they access personal data on behalf of the Corporation, they shall act as Data Processors and shall be subject to contractual obligations of security, confidentiality and purpose limitation.
Donors, cooperation agencies, funders, strategic partners and their representatives or contact persons To manage cooperation and funding relationships; to formulate, implement, supervise and evaluate the project; to coordinate activities; to prepare and submit technical, administrative and financial reports; to evidence compliance with indicators, targets and contractual obligations; to respond to audits and requests; to maintain institutional communications and to manage future cooperation opportunities. Reports must use anonymized or aggregated information, unless identifying individuals is necessary, authorized and legally permitted.
Users of websites, electronic forms, digital channels and other means of contact related to the project To respond to requests for information; to manage registrations, enquiries and communications; to facilitate access to content, services and activities; to verify the operation and security of digital channels; to prevent fraud, attacks or improper use; to carry out statistical analysis of their use and to improve accessibility, functionality and user experience.
Petitioners, enquirers, complainants, whistleblowers, claimants and people exercising their rights as data subjects To receive, identify, record, process and respond to petitions, enquiries, complaints, claims, reports and requests for access, updating, rectification, deletion or withdrawal of authorization; to verify the identity and standing of the person submitting the request; to gather and retain the corresponding supporting documents; to guarantee the traceability of the procedure; to adopt corrective measures and to respond to requests from the competent authorities.
People connected with information security incidents or possible breaches of this Policy To identify, analyse, document, contain, mitigate and resolve security incidents; to determine the information and the people affected; to adopt preventive and corrective measures; to make any appropriate communications; to notify the competent authorities where there is a duty to do so; to handle claims and to preserve evidence for the protection of data subjects' rights and the defence of the Corporation's legitimate interests.

General applicable purposes

Without prejudice to the specific purposes described above, and provided they are compatible with the existing relationship with each data subject, personal data may be processed in order to:

  1. Comply with the legal, regulatory, contractual, administrative, accounting, tax and audit obligations related to the project.
  2. Respond to requests from administrative, judicial, migration, oversight or rights protection authorities, only where they have competence and legal grounds to make them.
  3. Manage the security of the information systems, platforms, applications, files and other tools used.
  4. Maintain records of the authorizations given, privacy notices, requests submitted and processing operations carried out.
  5. Prevent, detect, investigate and manage possible fraud, unauthorized access, misuse, loss of information and security incidents.
  6. Produce statistics, analyses, studies, assessments, indicators and reports on the implementation and impact of the project, applying anonymization or aggregation measures wherever individual identification is not necessary.
  7. Carry out monitoring, evaluation, quality control, improvement and accountability activities.
  8. Exercise or defend rights in administrative, judicial, arbitration or out-of-court proceedings and retain the information required as evidence.
  9. Comply with internal policies on records management, archiving, retention and secure disposal of information.
  10. Develop other institutional programmes, projects or initiatives aimed at promoting, protecting and enforcing the rights of LGBTIQ+ people, only where this purpose has been notified, is compatible with the original purpose and is covered by the authorization given or by other legal grounds. Where the new use is incompatible with or exceeds the original authorization, a new authorization must be obtained.
  11. Collect and process, subject to express authorization, location or geolocation data in order to monitor migration journeys, identify risks and points of interest and contribute to users' guidance and safety. Real-time tracking shall be voluntary, may be deactivated and shall be subject to reinforced measures of security, confidentiality and restricted access.
  12. Design, promote and implement plans, programmes, projects and mechanisms that contribute to the recognition of sexual and gender diversity and to the promotion, protection and guarantee of the rights of LGBTIQ+ people, from differential, intersectional, territorial, gender and human mobility perspectives.
  13. Support, accompany and strengthen the processes, capacities and initiatives of social organizations, collectives, networks, leaders and defenders of the rights of LGBTIQ+ people, especially those in situations of human mobility.
  14. Design and deliver training, capacity-building, awareness-raising and outreach processes on sexual and gender diversity, human mobility, non-discrimination and the rights of LGBTIQ+ people, aimed at social organizations, public or private institutions, public officials, communities and society at large.
  15. Carry out follow-up, monitoring and evaluation of public policies, programmes, projects, institutional pathways and measures aimed at guaranteeing the rights of LGBTIQ+ people and of those in situations of human mobility.
  16. Prepare, coordinate and support studies, research, assessments, profiling exercises and reports that make it possible to understand the circumstances, needs, risks, barriers to accessing rights and experiences of LGBTIQ+ people, particularly in contexts of migration, refuge, displacement and other forms of human mobility.
  17. Formulate recommendations, proposals and advocacy actions aimed at promoting the creation, amendment or strengthening of rules, public policies, protocols, referral pathways and institutional practices relating to sexual and gender diversity, human mobility and the rights of LGBTIQ+ people.
  18. Promote and support the incorporation of gender, sexual orientation, gender identity and expression, sex characteristics, human mobility, intersectionality and do-no-harm perspectives into the formulation, regulation, implementation, monitoring and evaluation of planning instruments, public policies, programmes and projects.
  19. Generate statistical information, indicators and knowledge products to support decision-making, the formulation of public policies, accountability and advocacy actions, using anonymized or aggregated information where identifying data subjects is not necessary.

Under no circumstances may the personal data collected be used to discriminate against, persecute, stigmatize, criminalize, revictimize or expose data subjects to risk. Nor may they be disclosed to authorities, organizations or third parties for purposes incompatible with the provision of support, the protection of rights and the legitimate purposes previously notified, unless there is a valid legal or judicial mandate, whose validity must be verified before the information is provided.

9Authorization for the processing of personal data

Caribe Afirmativo shall process personal data after obtaining the free, prior, express and informed authorization of the data subject, except in cases where such authorization is not required by law. Authorization must be obtained before personal data are collected or, at the latest, at the same time as their collection, after communicating the specific purposes of processing, the data subject's rights and the mechanisms available to exercise them.

Where processing is carried out through information systems, digital platforms, applications, forms, databases or any other technological or documentary tool used in the project, the data subject must know and accept this Policy and give the corresponding authorization, where required.

Acceptance of this Policy by participating entities or organizations, technology providers, contractors or other legal persons involved in the project shall constitute an expression of their commitment to comply with the obligations corresponding to them as Data Controllers, Data Processors or third-party recipients of the information, according to the role they play. Such acceptance shall not replace the authorization to be given by the natural person who is the data subject. The Corporation shall have appropriate mechanisms in place to obtain authorization directly from the data subject or, where applicable, from their successors in interest, legal representatives, attorneys-in-fact or persons entitled to act on their behalf.

Authorization may be recorded in a physical or electronic document, in forms, acceptance checkboxes, recordings, data messages or any other format or mechanism that reasonably evidences that the data subject knew this Policy, was informed of the purposes of processing and expressed their will to authorize it.

9.1. Evidence of authorization

Authorization may be given in writing, verbally or through unequivocal conduct that reasonably allows the conclusion that the data subject consented to the processing. Silence, inactivity or failure to respond may under no circumstances be treated as unequivocal conduct or understood as authorization. The Corporation shall retain evidence of the authorizations obtained and shall keep the physical, electronic, technical or technological records that make it possible to evidence, as a minimum, the identity of the person who gave the authorization, the date and means used to obtain it, the purposes notified and the version of the Policy or privacy notice brought to their attention.

Where authorization is obtained by an organization acting as Data Processor, that organization must retain or make available to the Corporation the corresponding supporting records, in accordance with the instructions issued and with the applicable agreement, contract or legal instrument.

9.2. Authorization for the processing of sensitive data

The processing of sensitive data is prohibited, except where one of the exceptions provided for in article 6 of Law 1581 of 2012 applies. Where processing is based on the data subject's authorization, the Corporation or the Data Processor organization must:

  1. Inform the data subject that, because sensitive data are involved, they are not obliged to authorize their processing.
  2. Explicitly identify in advance which of the data to be processed are sensitive.
  3. Clearly, specifically and intelligibly state the purposes for which they will be processed.
  4. Obtain the data subject's explicit authorization by a means that evidences that it was given.
  5. Apply reinforced measures of security, confidentiality, restricted access and limited retention.

No person shall be obliged to provide sensitive data, and access to the project's general activities or services may not be made conditional on providing such information, except where the data are strictly necessary to provide a specific service requested by the data subject or to adopt a measure aimed at protecting their rights.

For information purposes, subparagraph (c) of article 6 of Law 1581 of 2012 permits the processing of sensitive data where it is carried out, in the course of legitimate activities and with due safeguards, by a foundation, non-governmental organization, association or other not-for-profit body whose purpose is political, philosophical, religious or trade union related, provided that the processing relates exclusively to its members or to persons who maintain regular contact with the organization by reason of its purpose.

The application of this exception must be assessed restrictively and in light of the specific circumstances of each processing operation. In any case, sensitive data processed on this basis may not be disclosed to third parties without the data subject's authorization. The fact that Caribe Afirmativo or the participating organizations belong to the not-for-profit sector does not release them from compliance with the principles, duties and protection measures established by applicable law.

9.3. Authorization for the processing of data of children and adolescents

The processing of personal data of children and adolescents is prohibited, except where the data are public in nature or where the processing serves their best interests, respects their prevailing rights and complies with the applicable constitutional and legal conditions.

Where processing is appropriate:

  1. Authorization must be given by the person legally empowered to represent the child or adolescent.
  2. Their right to be heard must be guaranteed and their views taken into account, having regard to their level of maturity, autonomy and capacity to understand the processing.
  3. The representative and the child or adolescent must be informed, in clear language appropriate to their age, of which data will be collected, the purposes of processing and the rights available to them.
  4. They must be informed that answering questions relating to sensitive data or to information about children and adolescents is optional.
  5. Reinforced measures must be adopted to guarantee the security, confidentiality and restricted use of the information.
  6. Where sensitive data are processed, the obligations set out in the previous subsection shall also be met.

9.4. Cases in which authorization is not required

In accordance with article 10 of Law 1581 of 2012, it shall not be necessary to obtain the data subject's authorization in the following cases:

  1. Where the information is required by a public or administrative body in the exercise of its legal functions or by court order.
  2. Where the data are public in nature.
  3. In cases of medical or public health emergency.
  4. Where processing is authorized by law for historical, statistical or scientific purposes.
  5. Where the data relate to the civil registry of persons.

The absence of a duty to obtain authorization does not release the Corporation or the Data Processor organizations from compliance with the principles, obligations and protection measures established by law and by this Policy. Where an authority requests information that is private, semi-private, confidential or sensitive, the Corporation must first verify the identity and competence of the authority, the legal basis, the purpose and the scope of the request, as well as the necessity and proportionality of the data requested. Disclosure shall be limited to the information strictly necessary, and a record shall be kept of the request and of the response provided.

9.5. Validity of the authorization

Authorization shall remain valid for the period that is reasonable and necessary to fulfil the purposes notified to the data subject and the applicable legal, contractual, administrative, accounting, documentary or legal defence obligations. Data subjects may request the withdrawal of their authorization and the deletion of their personal data at any time, except where a legal or contractual duty requires their retention or where processing is necessary for the recognition, exercise or defence of a right.

Withdrawal may be total, where the person requests the cessation of all authorized purposes, or partial, where it relates only to certain purposes. In the latter case, the Corporation may continue to carry out the processing that remains authorized or that is covered by other legal grounds. Once the purposes of processing have been fulfilled and the legal or contractual retention periods have expired, the data shall be deleted, anonymized or subjected to mechanisms preventing the identification of data subjects, in accordance with the policies on records management and secure disposal of information.

9.6. Privacy notice

The Privacy Notice is the verbal or written communication, produced in physical or electronic format or through any other suitable means, by which Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller, informs data subjects of the existence of this Policy, the essential characteristics of the processing of their personal data and the mechanisms available to know and consult it.

The Privacy Notice shall be made available to the data subject before or at the time of collecting their personal data. Where collection takes place through an information system, platform, application, form or any other technological tool used in the project, the notice shall be available before the person completes their registration, accepts this Policy and gives authorization for the processing of their data, where such authorization is required. The publication or communication of the Privacy Notice does not replace the obligation to obtain the data subject's authorization in the cases required by law. The Privacy Notice shall contain, as a minimum, the following information:

  1. The name or legal name and the contact details of Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller.
  2. The processing to which the personal data will be subject and the purposes for which they will be collected and used.
  3. The rights available to the data subject.
  4. The mechanisms available to know and consult the Personal Data Processing Policy, as well as to be informed of any substantial changes to it or to the Privacy Notice.
  5. The channels available for submitting enquiries, petitions, complaints or claims and for exercising the rights of access, knowledge, updating, rectification, deletion and withdrawal of authorization.
  6. Where sensitive data are collected, an express statement that the data subject is not obliged to authorize their processing, together with the identification of the sensitive data requested and the specific purposes for which they will be used.

As a due diligence measure, the Privacy Notice may be made available in the technological and documentary tools used for the implementation of the project and, in particular, may be presented before registration and before acceptance of this Policy. It may also be communicated through physical or electronic forms, data messages, emails, QR codes, links, information materials or other means that allow data subjects to become aware of it in good time.

10Rights of data subjects

In accordance with article 8 of Law 1581 of 2012, the data subjects whose personal data are processed by Corporación Caribe Afirmativo shall have the following rights:

  1. To know, update and rectify their personal data before the Corporation, as Data Controller, or before the organizations acting as Data Processors. This right may be exercised, among other cases, in relation to data that are partial, inaccurate, incomplete, fragmented or outdated, that may be misleading, or whose processing is expressly prohibited or has not been authorized.
  2. To request evidence of the authorization given, except where authorization is not required for the processing, in accordance with the exceptions provided for in article 10 of Law 1581 of 2012.
  3. To be informed, upon request, of the use made of their personal data, the purposes for which they have been processed and, where appropriate, the Data Processor organizations or third parties to whom they have been disclosed.
  4. To lodge complaints with the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio for possible breaches of Law 1581 of 2012 and of any rules amending, supplementing, regulating or complementing it, once the enquiry or complaint procedure before the Corporation has been exhausted.
  5. To withdraw authorization or request the deletion of personal data where the processing does not respect constitutional and statutory principles, rights and guarantees. They may also request withdrawal where there is no legal or contractual duty requiring the information to be retained or its processing to continue.
  6. To access their personal data free of charge where they have been processed, in accordance with the terms and conditions established by applicable law.
  7. To request the correction or deletion of location or geolocation data, as well as to voluntarily deactivate real-time tracking functionalities, without this preventing access to the project's other services, except where location is essential to provide a specific service requested by the data subject.
  8. To know the identity and contact details of the Corporation, as well as the channels available for submitting enquiries, petitions, complaints and claims relating to the processing of their personal data.

The exercise of these rights shall be free of charge and may not give rise to retaliation, discrimination, unjustified suspension of support or undue restrictions on access to the activities or services offered under the project.

11Procedure for handling enquiries, petitions, complaints and claims

Data subjects, their successors in interest or their representatives may submit enquiries, petitions, complaints or claims relating to the processing of their personal data at any time. The request must contain, as a minimum:

  • The data subject's full name.
  • Identity document.
  • Contact details for receiving the response.
  • A clear description of the request.
  • Any documents considered relevant, where necessary.

Caribe Afirmativo may verify the identity of the requester before providing information or acting on the request, in order to protect the confidentiality of personal data.

11.1. Service channels

Enquiries, petitions, complaints and claims may be submitted through any of the following channels:

Address
Carrera 60 No. 58-70, Barranquilla, Colombia
Email
administracion@caribeafirmativo.lgbt
gestion@caribeafirmativo.lgbt
Opening hours
Business days, 8:00 a.m. to 5:00 p.m.

11.2. Response times

Enquiries shall be answered within ten (10) business days of receipt. If it is not possible to answer them within that period, the data subject shall be informed before it expires, stating the reason for the delay and the date on which they will receive the response, which may not exceed a further five (5) business days.

Complaints relating to the updating, correction or deletion of data, the withdrawal of authorization or an alleged breach of personal data protection rules shall be dealt with within fifteen (15) business days of receipt.

Where a complaint is incomplete, Caribe Afirmativo shall ask the interested party to complete it. If two (2) months elapse without the required information being received, the request shall be deemed to have been abandoned. If for any reason it is not possible to respond to the complaint within the statutory period, the requester shall be informed in good time of the reasons for the delay and the date on which the response will be issued, which may not exceed eight (8) business days following the expiry of the original period.

Paragraph. Caribe Afirmativo invites data subjects to first submit their enquiries, petitions, complaints or claims through the service channels established in this Policy, in order to provide a timely response and to seek a direct and effective solution to concerns relating to the processing of their personal data. This does not limit the exercise of the rights recognized to data subjects by law or any action that may be brought before the competent authority in accordance with current legislation.

12Duties of the Corporation as Data Controller

Corporación Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller, shall comply with the following duties, without prejudice to the other obligations set out in Law 1581 of 2012, Decree 1074 of 2015, this Policy and any other provisions governing the processing of personal data:

  1. Guarantee data subjects, at all times, the full and effective exercise of the fundamental right of habeas data.
  2. Request and retain evidence of the authorization given by the data subject, except where the processing falls within one of the cases in which the law does not require authorization.
  3. Inform data subjects clearly, in advance and sufficiently of the purposes of collection and processing, the rights available to them and the channels available to exercise those rights.
  4. Expressly state that answering questions relating to sensitive data or to information about children and adolescents is optional, where applicable.
  5. Retain personal data under appropriate security conditions to prevent their tampering, loss, destruction, consultation, use, circulation, disclosure or unauthorized or fraudulent access.
  6. Ensure that the information provided to Data Processor organizations is truthful, complete, accurate, up to date, verifiable and understandable.
  7. Promptly inform Data Processor organizations of any change, updating, rectification, deletion or restriction relating to data previously provided, and adopt the measures necessary to keep the information up to date.
  8. Rectify personal data where they are incorrect, inaccurate or incomplete and communicate the correction to the Data Processor organizations that have access to them.
  9. Provide Data Processor organizations only with personal data whose processing is authorized by the data subject or covered by other legal grounds.
  10. Require Data Processor organizations, technology providers and sub-processors to comply on an ongoing basis with the conditions of security, privacy, confidentiality and restricted access established in this Policy and in the corresponding legal instruments.
  11. Select Data Processors that offer sufficient guarantees to implement appropriate technical, human, administrative and organizational measures for the protection of information.
  12. Handle the enquiries, petitions, complaints and claims submitted by data subjects within the time limits established by applicable law and by this Policy.
  13. Adopt and maintain internal policies, manuals and procedures that guarantee compliance with the personal data protection regime and, in particular, the handling of enquiries and complaints, the management of incidents and the exercise of data subjects' rights.
  14. Inform Data Processor organizations where certain information is contested by the data subject and the corresponding procedure has not been concluded.
  15. Inform the data subject, upon request, of the use made of their personal data, the purposes pursued and, where appropriate, the Data Processor organizations or third parties to whom they have been disclosed.
  16. Report to the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio any breaches of security codes and the existence of risks in the administration of information, in the cases and on the terms established by applicable law.
  17. Comply with the instructions and requirements issued by the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio and by other competent authorities.
  18. Apply reinforced protection measures to sensitive data, data of children and adolescents, and information on sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, migration status, health, experiences of violence and location or geolocation data.
  19. Limit access to personal data to the staff and organizations that need to know them in order to carry out previously defined and authorized functions.
  20. Assess the risks to data subjects' rights and freedoms before implementing new tools, functionalities or processing operations involving sensitive data, real-time tracking, profiling, automated decisions or large volumes of information.
  21. Implement privacy by design and by default measures in the information systems, platforms, applications, forms, databases and other technological or documentary tools used in the project.
  22. Verify that international transmissions and transfers of personal data comply with applicable legal requirements and are supported by contracts, agreements, clauses or other legal instruments offering sufficient guarantees.
  23. Use, wherever possible, anonymized or aggregated information, or information stripped of identifying elements, when producing statistics, studies, assessments, results reports and reports addressed to cooperation agencies, funders or third parties.
  24. Retain personal data only for the period that is reasonable and necessary to fulfil the purposes notified and the applicable legal or contractual obligations and, thereafter, proceed to their deletion, anonymization or secure disposal.
  25. Document the measures adopted to comply with this Policy and retain evidence demonstrating the appropriate management of personal data and of the risks associated with their processing.

Where Corporación Caribe Afirmativo directly determines the purposes and essential means of processing, it shall act as Data Controller and must fully comply with the duties set out above. If, in relation to a specific operation, it acts on behalf of and under the instructions of another entity, it must also observe the duties corresponding to Data Processors.

13Data Processors

In carrying out its functions, Caribe Afirmativo works with not-for-profit organizations, cooperation agencies, institutions and other partners located in different countries that take part in the implementation of the project and share the aim of assisting, supporting and protecting the rights of LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility. These entities shall be referred to as participating organizations.

Participating organizations may support activities such as providing guidance and services to users, recording information in the tools used to manage the project, following up on cases, identifying risks, activating protection pathways, making referrals and evaluating the results obtained.

Where a participating organization processes personal data on behalf of the Corporation and following its instructions, it shall act as a Data Processor. In such cases, processing shall be governed by contracts, agreements, cooperation arrangements or other legal instruments establishing the authorized purposes, the confidentiality and security obligations, the restrictions on access to and use of the information and the measures necessary to guarantee data subjects' rights.

The Corporation may also engage trusted technology providers to supply hosting, storage, support, maintenance, communications, backup, security or operation services for the tools used. Where these providers access personal data on behalf of the Corporation, they shall act as Data Processors and may use the information only to provide the contracted services and in accordance with the instructions issued.

The Corporation shall not provide information that directly identifies beneficiaries or users to participating organizations, cooperation agencies, funders or third parties for their own, commercial or otherwise incompatible purposes. Reports on the implementation and results of the project shall use, wherever possible, anonymized or aggregated information or information stripped of elements that identify individuals.

Where it is necessary to disclose personal data in order to make a referral, coordinate services, activate a protection pathway or provide a requested service, only the data that are adequate, relevant and strictly necessary shall be shared, subject to the data subject's prior authorization or on other legal grounds. If the disclosure involves an international transmission or transfer, the safeguards provided for by law and by the legal instruments signed between the organizations involved shall apply.

13.1. Duties of the Data Processor organizations

Participating organizations, technology providers and other third parties acting as Data Processors must comply with the following duties, without prejudice to the other obligations established in Law 1581 of 2012, Decree 1074 of 2015, this Policy, the instructions issued by the Corporation and applicable law:

  1. Guarantee data subjects, at all times, the full and effective exercise of the right of habeas data.
  2. Process personal data only on behalf of the Corporation, for the authorized purposes and following its documented instructions.
  3. Retain the information under appropriate security conditions to prevent its tampering, loss, destruction, consultation, use, disclosure or unauthorized or fraudulent access.
  4. Promptly update, rectify or delete personal data on the terms established by law and by the Corporation's instructions.
  5. Update the information communicated by the Corporation within five (5) business days of receipt.
  6. Handle the enquiries and complaints submitted by data subjects, or promptly forward them to the Corporation, in accordance with the procedure and time limits established in this Policy and by applicable law.
  7. Adopt internal policies, manuals or procedures that guarantee the appropriate processing of personal data and, in particular, the handling of enquiries and complaints, the management of incidents and the control of access to information.
  8. Record in the database the note “complaint in progress” where a complaint meeting the legal requirements is submitted, for as long as is necessary to resolve it.
  9. Include the note “information under judicial review” where they are notified by a competent authority of the existence of judicial proceedings relating to the quality or accuracy of the personal data.
  10. Refrain from circulating information contested by the data subject where its blocking has been ordered by the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio or by another competent authority.
  11. Allow access to the information only to expressly authorized staff who need to know it in order to carry out their assigned functions.
  12. Ensure that the persons authorized to process the data are bound by confidentiality commitments, including after the end of their employment, contractual, professional or cooperation relationship.
  13. Keep records that make it possible to identify who accessed, consulted, modified, downloaded, transmitted or deleted information, where the nature of the tool used allows it.
  14. Inform the Corporation immediately, through the established channels, of any loss, unauthorized access, security breach or incident that may compromise personal data.
  15. Report to the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio any breaches of security codes and risks in the administration of information, where this obligation applies directly to them, without prejudice to informing the Corporation beforehand.
  16. Comply with the instructions and requirements issued by the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio and by other competent data protection authorities.
  17. Refrain from supplying, transferring, disclosing, commercializing or using personal data for their own purposes or for purposes other than those authorized.
  18. Refrain from engaging sub-processors or allowing other third parties to access the information without the Corporation's prior authorization and without requiring equivalent obligations of protection, security and confidentiality.
  19. Delete or return to the Corporation the personal data once the service, agreement or activity that justified their processing has ended, unless a legal obligation requires their retention.
  20. Cooperate with the Corporation in carrying out audits, risk assessments, incident investigations and other activities intended to verify compliance with this Policy.

The mere participation of an organization, entity or partner in the project does not automatically confer on it the status of Data Processor; that status depends on the organization processing personal data on behalf of the Corporation and following its instructions. Where an organization independently determines the purposes and essential means of processing, it shall act as Data Controller in respect of those operations and must directly comply with the corresponding obligations. If the same organization acts simultaneously as Data Controller and Data Processor in different processing operations, it must observe the duties applicable to each role.

Organizations or providers located outside Colombia must comply, in respect of the data received on behalf of the Corporation, with the obligations established in the legal instruments signed, the safeguards set out in this Policy and applicable Colombian law, without prejudice to compliance with the legislation of the country in which they are established.

14International transfers and transmissions of personal data

The implementation of the project “Building Bridges, Weaving Paths: Promoting Actions for the Enforcement of Rights and the International Protection of LGBTIQ+ People in Situations of Human Mobility in Ibero-America” requires the collaboration of not-for-profit organizations, institutions, partners, cooperation agencies and providers located in different countries. Accordingly, Corporación Caribe Afirmativo may transmit or transfer personal data to recipients located inside or outside Colombia, where this is necessary to pursue the notified purposes, to guarantee services and protection for participants, to coordinate the project's activities or to comply with legal and contractual obligations.

International information flows may arise, among other cases, from the participation of organizations established in Argentina, Brazil, Chile, Colombia, Ecuador, Guatemala, Honduras, Mexico, Peru and Venezuela, as well as other entities, not-for-profit organizations, institutions, partners, cooperation agencies or providers located in countries that subsequently join the project or take part in its activities. The above list is merely illustrative and does not limit the territorial scope of the project or the inclusion of new participants. In any case, before allowing access to, transmission or transfer of personal data to an organization located in another jurisdiction, the Corporation shall verify compliance with the applicable legal provisions.

International flows may also arise from the use of technology services for hosting, storage, communications, support, maintenance, backup, security or information processing that operate through infrastructure located in other countries. The use of a particular platform, application, information system or technology provider may change during the implementation of the project. For this reason, the rules set out in this section shall apply regardless of the technology used, the location of its infrastructure or the place from which the service is provided.

14.1. International transmission of personal data

An international transmission shall exist where the Corporation communicates personal data to a participating organization, technology provider or other Data Processor located outside Colombia so that it may carry out processing on behalf of and following the instructions of the Corporation. International transmission of personal data shall be formalized through a contract, agreement, cooperation arrangement, data protection clause or other legal instrument specifying:

  1. The scope of the processing.
  2. The activities that the Data Processor will carry out on behalf of the Corporation.
  3. The Data Processor's obligations towards the Corporation and towards data subjects.

Through that instrument, the Data Processor shall undertake to apply the obligations set out in this Policy, to process personal data in accordance with the purposes authorized by data subjects and to comply with applicable law. In addition, the Data Processor must assume, as a minimum, the following obligations:

  1. To carry out processing, on behalf of the Corporation, in accordance with the principles that protect personal data.
  2. To safeguard the security of the databases and other files containing personal information.
  3. To maintain confidentiality regarding the processing of personal data.

Where an international transmission takes place between the Corporation and a Data Processor so that the latter may carry out processing on the Corporation's behalf, and a contract meeting the above conditions exists, it shall not be necessary to notify the data subject individually of the transmission or to obtain additional authorization to carry it out. This does not release the Corporation or the Data Processor from respecting the purposes previously notified, guaranteeing data subjects' rights and complying with the principles and duties established by applicable law. Data subjects shall also be guaranteed access, upon request, to the corresponding information about the organizations acting as Data Processors under the project.

14.2. International transfer of personal data

An international transfer shall exist where the Corporation sends personal data to an organization or entity located in another country that receives them in order to act as Data Controller. International transfers of personal data shall be carried out in accordance with the rules established in article 26 of Law 1581 of 2012. Accordingly, the Corporation shall verify that the receiving country provides an adequate level of data protection or that the transfer falls within one of the exceptions provided for by law.

Where the receiving country does not provide an adequate level of protection and no legal exception applies, the transfer may take place only once the corresponding declaration of conformity has been obtained from the Superintendencia de Industria y Comercio. The inclusion of an organization in the project or its location in any of the countries mentioned above does not in itself authorize the transfer of personal data. The appropriateness of each transfer must be assessed taking into account the capacity in which the recipient will act, the country of destination, the purpose of the processing and the applicable legal provisions.

The Corporation shall not commercialize or supply personal data to participating organizations, cooperation agencies, funders, providers or third parties for commercial or advertising purposes or for purposes incompatible with those previously notified. Information shall be disclosed only where it is necessary for the project's legitimate purposes, where the data subject has given authorization or where other valid legal grounds exist.

15Information security measures

Corporación Caribe Afirmativo shall adopt reasonable and proportionate administrative, technical, human, physical and organizational measures to protect personal data against tampering, loss, destruction, unauthorized access, misuse, consultation, alteration, disclosure or any other form of unauthorized or fraudulent processing.

The measures implemented shall take into account the nature of the information, the purposes of processing and the risks that may arise for data subjects. Reinforced measures shall be applied to sensitive data and to information on sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, health, migration status, experiences of violence, applications for international protection and location or geolocation data.

Access to personal data shall be restricted to staff, contractors, participating organizations, technology providers and other Data Processors who need to know the information in order to carry out previously defined and authorized functions. Persons involved in processing shall be subject to confidentiality obligations and must apply the security policies, procedures and good practices established by the Corporation.

The Corporation shall require Data Processor organizations to implement appropriate measures to safeguard the information processed on its behalf, as well as to promptly report any incident that may affect the security, confidentiality, integrity or availability of personal data.

Security measures shall be reviewed and updated reasonably in line with the evolution of risks, the characteristics of the processing and changes in the information systems, platforms, applications and other technological or documentary tools used in the project.

16Entry into force of the Policy and database retention period

This Personal Data Processing Policy takes effect from the date of its update. Any substantial amendment shall be communicated to data subjects through the following link, where the policy will be available: https://tic-ca1.github.io/politica-tratamiento-datos-prisma/

The Corporation may also communicate amendments through its institutional channels, information systems, platforms or the other tools used for the implementation of the project. The databases processed by Corporación Caribe Afirmativo, as well as the personal data contained in them, shall remain in force for as long as is necessary to fulfil the purposes set out in this Policy and to meet the applicable legal or contractual obligations. Once that period has elapsed, the Corporation shall proceed to delete or anonymize the personal data or the databases, as appropriate. Once the purpose of processing has ceased, and provided that no legal or contractual duty justifies their retention, the Corporation shall adopt reasonable measures for their deletion, anonymization, blocking or secure disposal, as appropriate.
