II · International transmission of data

Preliminary considerations

This Agreement on the International Transmission of Personal Data between Data Controller and Data Processor (hereinafter, the Agreement) governs the processing and transmission of the personal data collected in the course of the project “Building Bridges, Weaving Paths: Promoting Actions for the Enforcement of Rights and the International Protection of LGBTIQ+ People in Situations of Human Mobility in Ibero-America” (hereinafter, the Project).

For the purposes of the Agreement, Corporación Caribe Afirmativo shall act as Data Controller and Data Importer, since it determines the purposes and conditions under which the information will be processed in the course of the Project. The partner entity shall act as Data Processor and Data Exporter and shall therefore carry out the collection, recording, organization, transmission and other operations entrusted to it by Caribe Afirmativo, solely for the authorized purposes and in accordance with the instructions issued by the latter.

The Parties acknowledge that the relationship governed by this Agreement constitutes processing of personal data on behalf of the Data Controller, and that the partner entity's location outside Colombian territory entails an international flow of information. Accordingly, the partner entity may not use the personal data for its own purposes or for purposes that are different from or incompatible with those defined by Caribe Afirmativo, nor dispose of them outside the terms established in this Agreement. Where the processing of personal data provided for in the Project is prohibited or not permitted under the law applicable in the partner entity's jurisdiction, that entity must refrain from using the tool and from collecting or transmitting personal data through it.

The Agreement takes as its reference the clauses contained in the Model Agreement for the International Transfer of Personal Data between Data Controller and Data Processor of the Ibero-American Data Protection Network (RIPD), adapted to the nature of the international transmission carried out under the Project and to the obligations applicable to the relationship between Data Controller and Data Processor.

Given that partner entities may be located in different countries, each entity shall be responsible for knowing and complying with the provisions applicable to it in its respective jurisdiction regarding the collection, processing and international transmission of personal data. Accordingly, the partner entity must adopt the measures necessary to ensure the lawfulness of the operations it carries out, comply with the requirements of its local legislation and inform Caribe Afirmativo, before beginning or continuing processing, of any requirement, restriction or impediment that may affect the transmission of the data or compliance with this Agreement.

Where the legislation applicable to the partner entity requires the adoption of contractual clauses, safeguards, authorizations or other specific mechanisms to permit the international circulation of personal data, these must be complied with by the partner entity and, where appropriate, incorporated into this Agreement or its annexes. In the case of entities located in Brazilian territory, the applicable provisions of the Lei Geral de Proteção de Dados Pessoais (LGPD) and the standard contractual clauses issued by the Autoridade Nacional de Proteção de Dados (ANPD) shall also be observed, on the terms set out in the corresponding annex.

This Agreement shall apply together with the Project's Personal Data Processing Policy, the authorizations given by data subjects and the annexes forming part of this instrument. The partner entity declares that it is familiar with those documents and undertakes to process personal data within the limits, purposes and conditions established therein.

1Purpose, parties, scope and definitions

1.1. Purpose

  1. The purpose of this Agreement is to establish the conditions applicable to the processing and international transmission of Personal Data carried out in the course of the Project, as well as to ensure compliance with the principles, duties and safeguards applicable to the protection of Personal Data, with particular emphasis on LGBTIQ+ people.
  2. The Agreement governs, in particular, the Processing carried out by the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller and Data Importer, in respect of the Personal Data collected and entered into the web tool provided for the Project.
  3. Any interpretation and application of this Agreement must have regard to these purposes and ensure that the Processing of Personal Data is carried out solely for the purposes established in the Project.

1.2. Parties to the Agreement

  1. Corporación Caribe Afirmativo, hereinafter “Caribe Afirmativo” or the “Corporation”, identified by NIT 900.321.082-6, is a not-for-profit entity domiciled in Colombia which, for the purposes of this Agreement, acts as Data Controller and Data Importer.
  2. The legal person located outside Colombia that completes the registration process in the web tool provided for the Project, supplies the information required and accepts this Agreement shall have the status of “Partner Entity” and shall act, for the purposes of the Processing governed here, as Data Processor and Data Exporter.
  3. The identification and contact information supplied by the Partner Entity during the registration process shall form an integral part of this Agreement.
  4. Acceptance of this Agreement during the registration process means that the Partner Entity is aware of and accepts the conditions established here and undertakes to comply with them for as long as it carries out Processing operations on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo.

1.3. Scope

This Agreement shall apply to the Processing and international transmission of Personal Data carried out by the Partner Entity on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo in the course of the Project, in accordance with the purposes, categories of data subjects, categories of Personal Data, Processing operations and other conditions specified in its annexes.

The Agreement shall apply from the moment the Partner Entity collects, accesses, records or carries out any operation on Personal Data on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo and for as long as it has access to or processes that information.

The annexes, the information supplied during registration and the documents expressly incorporated into this Agreement form an integral part of it.

1.4. Definitions

Agreement
This Agreement on the International Transmission of Personal Data between Data Controller and Data Processor, together with its annexes and other documents forming an integral part of it.
Anonymization
The application of measures of any nature aimed at preventing the identification or re-identification of a natural person without disproportionate effort.
Competent Supervisory Authority
The personal data protection authority with jurisdiction over the Partner Entity, as Data Exporter, or over Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Importer.
Cloud Computing
A model allowing convenient, on-demand access to a set of computing resources and services, such as networks, servers, storage, applications and services, which can be provisioned and released with limited administrative intervention by the service provider.
Consent
A freely given, specific, unambiguous and informed expression of the data subject's will by which they accept and authorize the Processing of the Personal Data concerning them.
Personal Data
Any information concerning an identified or identifiable natural person, expressed in numerical, alphabetical, graphic, photographic, alphanumeric, acoustic or any other form. A person shall be regarded as identifiable where their identity can be determined directly or indirectly, provided this does not require disproportionate time or effort.
Sensitive Personal Data
Data relating to the data subject's private life or whose misuse may give rise to discrimination or entail a serious risk for them. By way of illustration, Personal Data regarded as sensitive are those that may reveal racial or ethnic origin; religious, philosophical or moral beliefs or convictions; trade union membership; political opinions; data concerning health; sexual life, preference or orientation; as well as genetic or biometric data intended to uniquely identify a natural person.
Automated Individual Decisions
Decisions that produce legal effects concerning the data subject or significantly affect them and that are based solely on automated Processing intended to evaluate, without human intervention, certain personal aspects or to analyse or predict, among other things, their professional performance, economic situation, health, sexual preferences, reliability or behaviour.
Data Processor
A natural or legal person, public authority, service or body that processes Personal Data on behalf of the Data Controller. For the purposes of this Agreement, this status corresponds to the Partner Entity.
Standards
The Personal Data Protection Standards for Ibero-American States approved by the Ibero-American Data Protection Network (RIPD) in 2017.
Data Exporter
A natural or legal person, public authority, service, body or service provider located in a jurisdiction from which Personal Data are transmitted internationally. For the purposes of this Agreement, this status shall apply to the Partner Entity which, acting as Data Processor, processes and transmits Personal Data on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo.
Data Importer
A natural or legal person, public authority, service, body or service provider located in a jurisdiction other than that of the Data Exporter and which receives the Personal Data subject to an international transmission. For the purposes of this Agreement, this status shall apply to Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller located in Colombia.
Applicable Law
Colombian personal data protection legislation governing this Agreement, without prejudice to the mandatory rules applicable to the Partner Entity in the jurisdiction from which it carries out the Processing and the International Transmission of Personal Data.
Administrative, Physical and Technical Measures
Measures intended to prevent the damage, loss, alteration, destruction, access and, in general, any unlawful or unauthorized Processing of Personal Data, including where it occurs accidentally, and which are sufficient to guarantee their confidentiality, integrity and availability.
Data Controller
A natural or legal person, public authority, service or body that, alone or jointly with others, determines the purposes, means, scope and other essential aspects relating to the Processing of Personal Data. For the purposes of this Agreement, this status corresponds to Caribe Afirmativo.
Sub-processor
A natural or legal person engaged by the Data Processor to carry out certain Processing activities on Personal Data on behalf of the Data Controller.
Third-Party Beneficiaries
Data subjects whose Personal Data are subject to the International Transmission governed by this Agreement and to whom rights or safeguards are granted under its provisions, even though they are not Parties to the Agreement.
Data subject
The natural person to whom the Personal Data being processed relate.
Onward Transfer
A subsequent disclosure or transfer of the Personal Data covered by this Agreement, made by the Partner Entity to a third party other than Caribe Afirmativo, located in a different jurisdiction, in accordance with Caribe Afirmativo's instructions and with the safeguards provided for in this Agreement and in the Applicable Law.
Processing
Any operation or set of operations carried out by physical or automated means on Personal Data, including, by way of illustration and without limitation, their obtaining, access, recording, organization, structuring, adaptation, indexing, modification, extraction, consultation, storage, retention, preparation, transmission, transfer, dissemination, use, deletion and, in general, any use of or dealing with Personal Data.
International Transmission of Personal Data
A Processing operation involving the communication of Personal Data between a Data Controller and a Data Processor located in different jurisdictions, where the Data Processor carries out the Processing on behalf of and under the instructions of the Data Controller. For the purposes of this Agreement, it covers the operations carried out by the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, to collect, record and transmit to Caribe Afirmativo in Colombia the Personal Data processed in the course of the Project.
Personal Data Security Breach
Any damage, loss, alteration, destruction, access and, in general, any unlawful or unauthorized Processing of Personal Data, including where it occurs accidentally.

2Effects and invariability of the clauses

2.1. Amendment of the Agreement. Limits

  1. This Agreement establishes the conditions and safeguards applicable to the Processing and International Transmission of Personal Data carried out by the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller and Data Importer.
  2. The provisions of this Agreement constitute minimum conditions for the protection of the Personal Data being processed and may not be interpreted, amended or applied in a way that reduces the safeguards afforded to the data subject or allows the Partner Entity to carry out Processing operations outside the purposes and instructions established by Caribe Afirmativo.
  3. Caribe Afirmativo may incorporate into this Agreement additional conditions, measures or safeguards where these are necessary for the development of the Project, the protection of data subjects or compliance with applicable Colombian law, provided they do not reduce the level of protection established in this instrument.
  4. Where the legislation applicable in the Partner Entity's jurisdiction requires additional clauses, safeguards or mechanisms to permit the Processing or International Transmission of Personal Data, these must be observed by the Partner Entity and, where appropriate, incorporated into this Agreement or its annexes.

2.2. Hierarchy with the Applicable Law and interpretation

  1. This Agreement shall be governed by and interpreted in accordance with Colombian personal data protection legislation and, in particular, with Law 1581 of 2012, its implementing rules and any other provisions amending, supplementing or replacing it.
  2. No provision of this Agreement may be interpreted or applied in a way that limits the rights granted to data subjects by Colombian law or reduces the obligations and safeguards established for the protection of their Personal Data.
  3. The provisions of this Agreement are without prejudice to the mandatory obligations applicable to the Partner Entity under the law of the jurisdiction from which it carries out the Processing and the International Transmission of Personal Data.
  4. The Partner Entity shall be responsible for verifying and complying with the provisions applicable to it in its jurisdiction and must promptly inform Caribe Afirmativo of any provision, decision of an authority, restriction or legal impediment that may affect compliance with this Agreement or the International Transmission of the Personal Data.

2.3. Hierarchy with other agreements

In the event of a conflict between the provisions of this Agreement and any other agreement, term, condition or instrument entered into or accepted between Caribe Afirmativo and the Partner Entity, the provisions of this Agreement shall prevail in all matters relating to the Processing, protection and International Transmission of Personal Data. Where another applicable instrument establishes additional safeguards for the protection of Personal Data, these may apply on a complementary basis provided they do not contradict this Agreement or entail a reduction of data subjects' rights.

3Third-party beneficiaries

Data subjects whose Personal Data are subject to Processing and International Transmission under this Agreement shall have the status of Third-Party Beneficiaries and may invoke the provisions established in their favour and require compliance with them from the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, and/or from Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller and Data Importer, in accordance with the obligations corresponding to each Party.

4Description of the transmission or transmissions and their purposes

The details and characteristics of the transmission or transmissions and, in particular, the categories of Personal Data transmitted and the purposes for which they are transmitted are set out in Annex B to this Agreement.

5Accession clause

  1. Entities that complete the registration process provided in the Project's web tool, supply the information required to identify them and expressly accept this Agreement and the documents forming an integral part of it may accede to this Agreement. Accession shall be subject to validation and admission by Caribe Afirmativo.
  2. Once the registration has been validated by Caribe Afirmativo, the entity shall acquire the status of Partner Entity and shall be regarded as a Party to this Agreement, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, becoming subject to the rights, obligations, conditions and responsibilities established in this instrument and its annexes.
  3. The accession of the Partner Entity shall take effect from the validation of its registration by Caribe Afirmativo and shall not give rise to rights or obligations in respect of periods prior to that accession.

6Obligations of the Parties in the processing of personal data

6.1. Instructions

The Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, shall carry out the Processing of the Personal Data on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo, without holding any decision-making power over the purposes of the Processing, and shall limit its actions to the conditions established in this Agreement, its annexes, the Project's Personal Data Processing Policy and the instructions issued by Caribe Afirmativo.

The Partner Entity shall inform Caribe Afirmativo where it considers that an instruction is contrary to the law applicable to it or where there is a provision, decision of an authority or legal circumstance preventing it from complying.

6.2. Accountability principle

  1. Caribe Afirmativo shall establish the conditions necessary for the Processing carried out by Partner Entities to conform to the purposes of the Project and shall adopt reasonable measures to verify that they are able to comply with the obligations set out in this Agreement.
  2. The Partner Entity shall implement the measures and mechanisms necessary to demonstrate compliance with the obligations corresponding to it as Data Processor and Data Exporter, as well as with the instructions issued by Caribe Afirmativo.
  3. The Partner Entity shall periodically review and assess the effectiveness of the measures adopted to comply with this Agreement and must retain sufficient evidence to demonstrate such compliance when required by Caribe Afirmativo or by the Competent Supervisory Authority.

6.3. Purpose limitation principle

The Partner Entity may process the Personal Data only for the purposes established in Annex B and in accordance with the instructions issued by Caribe Afirmativo. Accordingly, it may not use, exploit, consult, retain, disclose or process the Personal Data for its own purposes or for purposes different from or incompatible with those provided for in the Project.

Any additional instruction from Caribe Afirmativo must relate to the purposes authorized for the Processing of the Personal Data.

6.4. Transparency

  1. Upon request, the Parties shall make a copy of this Agreement available to the data subject free of charge. In any event, the Partner Entity assumes on its own initiative the responsibility of informing data subjects of its existence. Those sections or annexes of the Agreement containing trade secrets or other confidential information, such as Personal Data of third parties or information that is confidential under the rules applicable to the Parties, must be excluded.
  2. The Partner Entity must cooperate with Caribe Afirmativo and supply it with the information necessary to handle data subjects' requests relating to the Processing carried out by the Partner Entity.
  3. The foregoing is without prejudice to the information and transparency duties that apply directly to each Party under the law applicable to it.

6.5. Accuracy and data minimization

  1. The Partner Entity must ensure, so far as possible, that the Personal Data collected and recorded in the tool correspond to the information supplied by the data subject and are limited to those necessary for the purposes established in the Project.
  2. Where the Partner Entity becomes aware that the Personal Data processed are inaccurate, incomplete, out of date or require rectification, it shall inform Caribe Afirmativo without undue delay and shall cooperate in adopting the measures necessary for their updating, rectification or deletion, as appropriate.

6.6. Security principle

  1. Caribe Afirmativo and the Partner Entity shall adopt, within the scope of their respective responsibilities, appropriate Administrative, Physical and Technical Measures to guarantee the confidentiality, integrity and availability of the Personal Data and to protect them against a Security Breach. In determining the level of security, account must be taken, among other factors, of the nature and sensitivity of the Personal Data, the characteristics of the Processing, the risks to data subjects and the technical conditions under which the Project is carried out. The applicable measures are specified in Annex C.
  2. Where the Partner Entity becomes aware of a Security Breach that affects or may affect the Personal Data processed under this Agreement, it must immediately adopt the reasonable measures available to it to contain the breach, mitigate its effects and prevent its recurrence.
  3. The Partner Entity shall notify Caribe Afirmativo of the Security Breach without undue delay and, in any event, within seventy-two (72) hours of becoming aware of it. The communication must state, so far as possible, the nature of the incident, the categories of Personal Data affected, the approximate number of data subjects and records compromised, its possible consequences and the measures adopted or proposed to contain it and mitigate its effects.
  4. Where it is not possible to provide all the information at once, the Partner Entity shall initially supply the information available to it and shall supplement the communication as it obtains further information, without undue delay.
  5. The Partner Entity shall cooperate with Caribe Afirmativo in managing the incident and shall supply the information necessary for the Corporation to determine and comply with the reporting, communication or other obligations owed to data subjects and to the Competent Supervisory Authorities.

6.7. Processing under the authority of the Partner Entity and confidentiality principle

  1. The Partner Entity shall ensure that the persons acting under its authority have access to the Personal Data only where strictly necessary to carry out the activities authorized under the Project and that they process the data in accordance with this Agreement and the instructions issued by Caribe Afirmativo.
  2. The Partner Entity shall ensure that every person authorized to access or process the Personal Data is subject to confidentiality obligations, which shall remain in force even after the end of their participation in the Project, their relationship with the Partner Entity or the term of this Agreement.

6.8. Processing of sensitive personal data

  1. Where the Processing involves Sensitive Personal Data, the Partner Entity must apply the additional measures, restrictions and safeguards provided for in this Agreement, in Annex C and in the instructions issued by Caribe Afirmativo, taking particular account of the risks that their Processing may pose to data subjects.
  2. Where the Processing involves Personal Data of children or adolescents, the Parties must give priority to their best interests and to the protection of their rights, in accordance with the applicable provisions and the relevant international protection instruments.

6.9. Onward transfers

  1. The Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, may disclose the Personal Data covered by this Agreement to a third party only where there is a prior documented instruction from Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller and Data Importer, and provided such disclosure is permitted under the Applicable Law.
  2. Where the third party is located in a jurisdiction other than that from which the Partner Entity carries out the Processing, the disclosure may take place only if the third party is bound by the provisions of this Agreement or assumes equivalent obligations and safeguards for the protection of the Personal Data. Where this is not the case, the Onward Transfer may be carried out only if: (i) it is addressed to a jurisdiction with an adequate level of personal data protection recognized under the rules applicable to the operation; (ii) the recipient third party offers adequate safeguards for the protection of the Personal Data; (iii) it is necessary for the establishment, exercise or defence of claims in administrative, regulatory or judicial proceedings; or (iv) it is necessary to protect the vital interests of the data subject or of another natural person.
  3. Every Onward Transfer must respect the purposes for which the Personal Data were collected and shall be subject to the other safeguards provided for in this Agreement. Caribe Afirmativo's authorization or instruction to carry out an Onward Transfer shall not release the Partner Entity from compliance with the obligations corresponding to it as Data Processor and Data Exporter, nor entitle it to use the Personal Data for its own purposes or for purposes other than those established in the Project.

6.10. Documentation and compliance

  1. The Parties must be able to demonstrate compliance with the obligations arising from this Agreement. In particular, the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, shall retain sufficient documentation of the Processing activities it carries out on behalf of and under the instructions of Caribe Afirmativo, which it shall make available to the latter and to the Competent Supervisory Authority upon request.
  2. The Partner Entity shall respond promptly and appropriately to Caribe Afirmativo's enquiries and requests relating to the Processing of Personal Data carried out under this Agreement.
  3. The Partner Entity shall make available to Caribe Afirmativo all the information necessary to demonstrate compliance with the obligations set out in this Agreement and, at the latter's request, shall allow for and contribute to audits of the Processing activities covered by the Agreement, at reasonable intervals or where there are indications of non-compliance. Caribe Afirmativo may carry out the audit directly or through an independent auditor. Where necessary and proportionate, audits may include the verification of the systems, procedures, facilities or other means used by the Partner Entity for the Processing of the Personal Data, with reasonable prior notice.
  4. The Parties shall make available to the Competent Supervisory Authority, when it so requires and in accordance with the applicable rules, the information relating to compliance with this Agreement and, in particular, the results of the audits carried out.

6.11. Duration of processing and deletion or return of the data

  1. The Processing of the Personal Data by the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, shall be carried out only during the period specified in Annex B to this Agreement and for as long as it is authorized to take part in the Project.
  2. Once the Processing activities entrusted to it have ended, the Partner Entity's participation in the Project has ceased or Caribe Afirmativo so requests, the Partner Entity must, in accordance with Caribe Afirmativo's instructions, securely delete all the Personal Data processed on its behalf and certify that deletion, or return the Personal Data to Caribe Afirmativo and securely delete any existing copies, where Caribe Afirmativo opts for the latter alternative. Until the Personal Data are deleted or returned, the Partner Entity shall continue to guarantee compliance with the obligations set out in this Agreement.
  3. Where the legislation applicable in the Partner Entity's jurisdiction prohibits the return or deletion of the Personal Data or requires their retention for a given period, the Partner Entity must inform Caribe Afirmativo of that circumstance and shall continue to guarantee compliance with this Agreement, limiting the Processing solely to the retention required by law and for the period established by that legislation.

7Use of sub-processors

7.1. Prior authorization of the sub-processor

  1. The Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, may not contract, engage or use a Sub-processor to carry out, in whole or in part, Processing activities on Personal Data on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo without the latter's prior, express and specific authorization.
  2. Where the Partner Entity needs to use a Sub-processor, it must request authorization from Caribe Afirmativo before the third party has access to the Personal Data or begins any Processing activity. The request must contain the information necessary to identify the Sub-processor, the Processing activities it will carry out, its location or jurisdiction, the Personal Data to which it will have access and the measures and safeguards provided for their protection.
  3. Caribe Afirmativo's authorization must be given expressly and may be recorded by electronic means. The absence of a reply to a request for authorization shall under no circumstances be understood as authorization to use the Sub-processor.
  4. The Partner Entity may not allow access to the Personal Data or entrust Processing activities to the Sub-processor until it has obtained the authorization provided for in this clause.

7.2. Contract with the sub-processor

  1. Where the Partner Entity, having obtained Caribe Afirmativo's authorization under clause 7.1, uses a Sub-processor to carry out specific Processing activities on Personal Data on behalf of Caribe Afirmativo, it must formalize that relationship by means of a written contract, including by electronic means, imposing on the Sub-processor personal data protection obligations equivalent to those assumed by the Partner Entity under this Agreement, particularly as regards purpose, confidentiality, security, handling of data subjects' rights, retention, deletion or return of the Personal Data, and Onward Transfers. The Partner Entity must ensure that the Sub-processor complies with the obligations applicable to it.
  2. The engagement of a Sub-processor must comply with the conditions set out in the clause on Onward Transfers where, by reason of the Sub-processor's location or of the Processing activities entrusted to it, an operation covered by that clause occurs.
  3. The Partner Entity shall provide Caribe Afirmativo, at its request, with a copy of the contract concluded with the Sub-processor and of any amendments to it. Where it is necessary to protect confidential information or Personal Data of third parties, the Partner Entity may redact or withhold that information before providing the copy, provided this does not prevent Caribe Afirmativo from verifying compliance with the obligations established in this Agreement.
  4. The Partner Entity shall remain fully liable to Caribe Afirmativo for the performance of the obligations entrusted to the Sub-processor. The authorization given by Caribe Afirmativo shall not limit or exclude that liability. The Partner Entity must inform Caribe Afirmativo, without undue delay, of any failure by the Sub-processor to comply with the personal data protection obligations assumed under the contract concluded with it.

8Rights of data subjects

  1. The Partner Entity shall notify Caribe Afirmativo without undue delay of any enquiry, petition, complaint, claim or request to exercise rights that it receives directly from a data subject in relation to the Personal Data processed under this Agreement. The Partner Entity shall not answer the request itself unless Caribe Afirmativo has expressly authorized it to do so, without prejudice to the obligations directly applicable to it under the rules of its own jurisdiction.
  2. The Partner Entity shall provide Caribe Afirmativo with the cooperation necessary to handle the enquiries, petitions, complaints, claims and requests by which data subjects exercise the rights available to them under the Applicable Law. To that end, it shall adopt the Administrative, Physical and Technical Measures provided for in Annex C, taking into account the nature of the Processing, and shall supply in good time the information and assistance necessary for Caribe Afirmativo to handle those requests.
  3. In complying with the obligations set out in the preceding subparagraphs, the Partner Entity shall follow the instructions issued by Caribe Afirmativo and shall refrain from any action that may limit, prevent or hinder the exercise of data subjects' rights.

9Complaints

  1. The Partner Entity shall inform data subjects, clearly, transparently and in an easily accessible manner, of the contact point and the channels provided by Caribe Afirmativo for submitting and handling enquiries, petitions, complaints, claims and other requests relating to the Processing of their Personal Data. Where the Partner Entity receives any such request directly, it must communicate and forward it to Caribe Afirmativo without undue delay and provide the cooperation necessary for it to be properly handled.
  2. In the event of a dispute between a data subject and one of the Parties concerning compliance with this Agreement, that Party shall do its utmost to resolve the matter amicably and in good time. The Parties shall keep each other informed of such disputes and shall, where appropriate, cooperate in good faith to resolve them.
  3. The Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, undertakes to accept and not to contest, where a data subject invokes a right available to them as a Third-Party Beneficiary under this Agreement, their decision to: (i) lodge a complaint with the competent data protection authority of the State of their habitual residence or place of work, or with the Competent Supervisory Authority; or (ii) bring the judicial proceedings available to them in relation to the Processing of their Personal Data.
  4. The Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, agrees to abide by the decisions and rulings that are binding under the Applicable Law or under the rules applicable in the jurisdiction concerned.

10Civil liability

  1. Each Party shall be liable to the other for the damages it causes as a result of failing to comply with the obligations corresponding to it under this Agreement.
  2. The Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, shall be liable to the data subject for the material or non-material damages that it or the Sub-processors it engages cause as a result of failing to comply with the obligations and safeguards established in this Agreement in favour of data subjects as Third-Party Beneficiaries. The foregoing is without prejudice to any liability that may correspond to Caribe Afirmativo, as Data Controller and Data Importer, under the Applicable Law.
  3. Where Caribe Afirmativo is held liable and must pay compensation for damages attributable, in whole or in part, to the failure of the Partner Entity or of a Sub-processor engaged by it to comply with its obligations, Caribe Afirmativo may claim from the Partner Entity reimbursement of that part of the compensation corresponding to its liability.
  4. Where Caribe Afirmativo and the Partner Entity are liable for the same damage caused to a data subject as a result of non-compliance with this Agreement, liability towards the data subject shall be determined in accordance with the Applicable Law and with any other rules applicable to the specific case.
  5. Where one of the Parties has paid compensation corresponding, in whole or in part, to damage attributable to the other Party, it may claim from the latter reimbursement of the proportion corresponding to its liability.
  6. The Partner Entity may not invoke the act, omission or non-compliance of a Sub-processor in order to exclude or limit its own liability towards Caribe Afirmativo or data subjects under this Agreement.

11Supervision by the Competent Supervisory Authority

  1. Each Party acknowledges the supervisory powers of the Competent Supervisory Authority over the Processing activities within its competence and undertakes to cooperate with it in the procedures or proceedings intended to verify compliance with the obligations set out in this Agreement and in the applicable rules.
  2. In particular, the Partner Entity, as Data Processor and Data Exporter, undertakes to respond in good time to the requests and enquiries of the Competent Supervisory Authority, to cooperate in the verification or audit proceedings it conducts and to comply with the orders, corrective measures and other decisions that are binding under the applicable rules. Where appropriate, it must demonstrate to that authority that the required measures have been adopted.
  3. The Partner Entity shall inform Caribe Afirmativo, without undue delay, of any request, investigation, proceeding, audit, measure or decision of a Competent Supervisory Authority relating to the Personal Data processed under this Agreement, unless a legal provision or an order of a competent authority prohibits it from making that communication.

12Local law and practices affecting compliance with the clauses

  1. The Parties confirm that, at the time of entering into this Agreement, they have made reasonable efforts to identify whether the Personal Data subject to Processing and International Transmission are subject, in the Partner Entity's jurisdiction, to any local legal provision or practice that goes beyond what is necessary and proportionate to safeguard legitimate public interest objectives and that may reasonably affect the protections, rights and safeguards granted to data subjects under this Agreement. On the basis of that assessment, the Parties confirm that they are not aware of any provisions or practices adversely affecting the protections, rights and safeguards established in this Agreement.
  2. The Partner Entity undertakes to notify Caribe Afirmativo immediately if, after this Agreement is entered into, any law, regulatory provision, decision of an authority or practice of its jurisdiction becomes applicable to it that may affect compliance with the obligations, protections, rights or safeguards provided for in this Agreement. Once that notification has been made, or where Caribe Afirmativo has reasonable grounds to consider that the Partner Entity can no longer comply with the obligations established in this Agreement, Caribe Afirmativo shall determine the administrative, physical, technical or contractual measures that are appropriate to remedy the situation and guarantee the protection of the Personal Data. It may also suspend the Processing and International Transmission of Personal Data where it considers that adequate safeguards cannot be ensured. In that case, Caribe Afirmativo may terminate this Agreement in accordance with clause 13.
  3. If a court, administrative authority, government agency or other competent authority requires either Party to disclose or use the Personal Data covered by this Agreement in a manner that would not otherwise be permitted by it, the Party concerned shall review the lawfulness of that request and shall challenge it if, after careful legal assessment, it concludes that there are reasonable grounds to consider the request unlawful under the applicable law and that it affects the rights and safeguards recognized in this Agreement. To the extent permitted by the applicable law, the Party concerned shall immediately inform the other Party of the receipt of such a request. If the Party concerned is prohibited from making that notification under the applicable law, it shall do its utmost, within the legally available mechanisms, to obtain a waiver or the lifting of the prohibition.

13Non-compliance with the clauses and termination of the contract

  1. The Partner Entity shall inform Caribe Afirmativo immediately where, for any reason, it is unable to comply with any of the obligations established in this Agreement.
  2. Should the Partner Entity fail to comply with the obligations imposed on it by this Agreement, Caribe Afirmativo may suspend the Processing and International Transmission of Personal Data until compliance with the corresponding obligations is restored or this Agreement is terminated. During the period of suspension, the Partner Entity shall refrain from carrying out new Processing activities on the Personal Data covered by the Agreement, except those strictly necessary to guarantee their retention, security and protection.
  3. Caribe Afirmativo shall be entitled to terminate this Agreement where: (i) it has suspended the Processing and International Transmission of Personal Data under the preceding subparagraph and compliance with this Agreement is not restored within a reasonable period and, in any event, within thirty (30) business days following the suspension; (ii) the Partner Entity substantially or persistently fails to comply with the obligations established in this Agreement; or (iii) the Partner Entity fails to comply with a binding decision or ruling of a court or of a Competent Supervisory Authority relating to the obligations corresponding to it under this Agreement. Where appropriate, Caribe Afirmativo shall report that non-compliance to the Competent Supervisory Authority.
  4. The Personal Data processed by the Partner Entity before the termination of this Agreement must, at Caribe Afirmativo's choice, be returned to it immediately or securely deleted in their entirety. The same obligation shall apply to copies of the Personal Data held by or under the control of the Partner Entity, which must certify their deletion to Caribe Afirmativo. Until the Personal Data are returned or deleted, the Partner Entity shall continue to guarantee compliance with the obligations set out in this Agreement.

Where the legislation applicable in the Partner Entity's jurisdiction prohibits the return or deletion of the Personal Data or requires their retention, the Partner Entity shall immediately inform Caribe Afirmativo of that circumstance, shall continue to guarantee compliance with this Agreement and shall process the Personal Data only to the extent and for the period required by that legislation.

14Governing law

This Agreement shall be governed by and interpreted in accordance with the personal data protection legislation of the Republic of Colombia, in particular Law 1581 of 2012, its implementing rules and any rules amending, supplementing or replacing them, without prejudice to compliance with the mandatory rules applicable to the Partner Entity in the jurisdiction from which it carries out the Processing and the International Transmission of Personal Data.

15Choice of forum and jurisdiction

  1. Any dispute between the Parties arising from the conclusion, interpretation, performance, compliance, non-compliance, termination or effects of this Agreement shall be submitted to the jurisdiction of the Republic of Colombia and resolved by the competent judicial authorities of the city of Barranquilla, in accordance with the Applicable Law.
  2. Without prejudice to the foregoing, data subjects, as Third-Party Beneficiaries, may bring the actions available to them against Caribe Afirmativo and/or the Partner Entity before the judicial authorities having jurisdiction under the applicable rules, including, where appropriate, those of their country of habitual residence, those of the Partner Entity's jurisdiction or those of the Republic of Colombia.
  3. The Parties agree to submit disputes arising between them to the jurisdiction provided for in subparagraph (a) of this clause, without that choice being interpreted as a limitation of the rights, complaint mechanisms or avenues of protection available to data subjects under the applicable rules.

16Annexes and electronic completion

  1. The Annexes form an integral part of this Agreement and contain the information necessary to identify the Partner Entity and to specify the conditions applicable to the Processing and International Transmission of Personal Data carried out under the Project.
  2. The information to be supplied by the Partner Entity shall be completed through the registration form provided in the Project's web tool. The data, declarations, selections and other information supplied during that process shall be regarded as incorporated into the corresponding Annex and shall form an integral part of this Agreement once the Partner Entity expresses its acceptance and its registration is validated by Caribe Afirmativo.
  3. The contents of the Annexes previously established by Caribe Afirmativo shall be made available to the Partner Entity for its information and acceptance during the registration process. Electronic acceptance of this Agreement shall also cover the Annexes applicable to the Partner Entity.
  4. The Partner Entity shall be responsible for the truthfulness, accuracy, completeness and updating of the information supplied through the registration form and must inform Caribe Afirmativo of any change that may affect the conditions under which it carries out the Processing or the International Transmission of Personal Data.
  5. Where, by reason of the Partner Entity's jurisdiction, the incorporation of standard contractual clauses, model clauses, additional safeguards or other specific instruments for the International Transmission of Personal Data is mandatory, these shall be incorporated into this Agreement through the corresponding Annex and shall form an integral part of it. The Partner Entity must inform Caribe Afirmativo of the existence and enforceability of such instruments before beginning the International Transmission of Personal Data.
  6. Where the applicable rules require that standard contractual clauses or any other instrument be incorporated in full, without amendment or subject to particular formalities, their incorporation and acceptance shall take place in accordance with those requirements, and the provisions of this Agreement may not be interpreted as excluding, modifying or contradicting their content.

  1. Full name or corporate name.
  2. Registered address.
  3. Country.
  4. Contact.
  5. Activities relating to the Personal Data subject to Processing and International Transmission under this Agreement. The Partner Entity consents to processing personal data in connection with the Project for the purpose of collecting the Personal Data of data subjects who voluntarily supply their information for the purposes of the Project; recording and entering that information into the web tool provided by Caribe Afirmativo; and carrying out the Processing operations strictly necessary for its transmission to Caribe Afirmativo in Colombia, in accordance with its instructions and without the Partner Entity being able to use the Personal Data for its own purposes or for purposes different from or incompatible with those established in the Project.
  6. Applicable rules in its jurisdiction.
  7. Competent Supervisory Authority.

BAnnex B. Description of the international transmission

Data subject
Voluntarily supplies their Personal Data after giving informed consent.

Partner Entity · Data Processor and Data Exporter
Collects the information supplied and authorized by the data subject and records it, through its authorized users, in the tool or Platform for the Registration, Information and Monitoring of Mobility in the Americas.

Caribe Afirmativo · Colombia · Data Controller and Data Importer
Processes the information in accordance with the purposes authorized by the data subject.

Categories of data subjects whose Personal Data are subject to International Transmission

LGBTIQ+ people in situations of forced displacement who receive support; users who operate the platform; representatives of the organizations; and, where applicable, third parties who may become identifiable through free-text fields or attachments entered into the tool. These categories correspond to those currently identified in the operation of the platform.

Categories of Personal Data subject to International Transmission

Identification data; contact details; sociodemographic data; information relating to the situation of human mobility, displacement and migration status; information on displacement routes; information on incidents of violence; protection needs; referrals and their outcome; approximate location of incidents; information contained in observations and free-text fields; attachments and evidence of informed consent. In respect of the users of the tool, the data processed include the username, institutional email address, assigned role and the country and organization groups to which the account belongs.

Sensitive Personal Data subject to International Transmission and restrictions or safeguards applied

Data relating to gender identity, sexual orientation, ethnic origin, disability, health information and other Sensitive Personal Data that may be contained in the structured fields, free-text fields or attachments of PRISMA may be processed. Gender identity, sexual orientation, ethnic group and other data associated with a person's identity shall be recorded according to their self-identification. Their Processing shall be subject to the data subject's informed consent, to the purposes established for the Project and to the measures and safeguards provided for in this Agreement and its Annexes.

Frequency of the International Transmission

Continuous, throughout the implementation of the Project, to the extent that the authorized users of the Partner Entities collect and record information in the tool or carry out the other authorized operations on the Personal Data.

Purposes of the International Transmission and subsequent Processing of the data

To characterize the human rights situation of LGBTIQ+ people in situations of human mobility, through the recording and analysis of sociodemographic information, displacement routes, incidents indicative of violence and international protection needs; to follow up on protection needs and on the referrals recorded; to produce statistical and aggregated information; and to support organizational strengthening, rights protection and local and regional advocacy, through the participation of not-for-profit entities and organizations dedicated to promoting, defending and protecting the rights of LGBTIQ+ people, in accordance with the purposes of the Project and the authorizations given by data subjects.

Period

For the term of this Agreement and for as long as necessary to fulfil the authorized purposes of the Processing, in accordance with Caribe Afirmativo's instructions and the applicable rules. Anonymization of the information and data subjects' right to have their personal data deleted shall take precedence.

Sub-processors

The Partner Entity may not use Sub-processors for the Processing of the Personal Data covered by this Agreement without the prior, express and specific authorization of Caribe Afirmativo, in accordance with clause 7.

CAnnex C. Administrative, physical and technical measures to guarantee the security of the data

In order to guarantee the security, confidentiality, integrity and availability of the Personal Data subject to Processing and International Transmission under this Agreement, the following measures are established:

C.1. Administrative measures

  1. Assignment of access accounts to each Partner Entity, the use of which is limited to the persons it designates and authorizes.
  2. Assignment of roles and access permissions according to the functions each user is to perform.
  3. Restriction of access to the Personal Data in accordance with the need-to-know principle and the permissions previously assigned.
  4. Logging of the relevant activities carried out by users, including operations involving the modification of information.
  5. Review and management of accounts and access permissions, including their modification or removal when they are no longer necessary.
  6. Limitation of operational access to the systems and to the Personal Data to authorized staff who require it for maintenance, support or operation tasks.
  7. Management and periodic application of security updates and fixes intended to mitigate vulnerabilities.
  8. Procedures for the identification, management and communication of security incidents or events that may result in the loss of or damage to the Personal Data.
  9. Documented recovery and continuity procedures for incidents affecting the availability of the information.

C.2. Physical measures

  1. Hosting of the information on infrastructure specifically dedicated to the provision of data storage and processing services.
  2. Restriction of physical access to the infrastructure on which the Personal Data are stored or processed to duly authorized staff.
  3. Protection of the media and infrastructure used for backups of the Personal Data.
  4. Implementation of measures intended to preserve the availability of the infrastructure and to allow the recovery of the information in the event of physical failures or incidents.

C.3. Technical measures

C.3.1. The tool

  1. Transmission of the Personal Data exclusively over HTTPS, using TLS 1.2 and TLS 1.3, Let's Encrypt certificates and HSTS.
  2. Configuration of the tool as a private instance, so that consulting information requires authentication and, without credentials, only the login screen can be reached.
  3. Creation of accounts from the administration profile and activation through a personal link valid for twenty-four hours, from which the password is set. Passwords are stored using the bcrypt cryptographic hash function and not in reversible plain text.
  4. Two-factor authentication using time-based one-time passwords (TOTP), available for all accounts.
  5. Access control based on three profiles: administration, reserved for Caribe Afirmativo and for the hosting provider's technical staff who require it for support; editing; and collaboration, which is the profile assigned to the Partner Entity's accounts.
  6. Privacy by default for records: what a collaboration account creates is restricted to that account and to the administration and editing profiles. Another account can access a record only where it is expressly shared with it, with viewing or editing permission, and solely within the tool.
  7. Activity logging of sign-in and sign-out and of the actions that modify information, including the creation, modification and deletion of records and changes to users and permissions, stating the action, the account, the description and the date and time.
  8. Daily encrypted backups of the database and of the files, retaining seven daily, four weekly and three monthly copies.
  9. Operation of the servers behind a virtual private network, with communication between them limited to expressly permitted addresses and ports.
  10. Access by the hosting provider to the production systems limited to a small group of technical and operations staff, through an authenticated VPN and according to maintenance and support needs.
  11. Hardening of the servers through host firewalls, request filtering and rate limiting, and blocking of addresses showing repeated failed access attempts.
  12. Web application firewall for the detection and monitoring of threats directed against the tool.
  13. Continuous application of security updates and vulnerability management. The application has undergone independent security audits in 2022, by Recurity Labs, and in 2024, by Assured, whose findings were addressed.
  14. Removal of deleted records, files and accounts from the live instance. Deleted content may remain in the encrypted backups for up to three months, while these are rotated.

C.3.2. The Partner Entity

  1. Use of each account solely by the persons designated by the Partner Entity, which shall keep an up-to-date list of those who use it, shall not share the credentials with other persons and shall change the password whenever one of them ceases to have access.
  2. Passwords of at least twelve characters, used only for the tool, not written down on visible media or saved in browsers on shared computers, and changed immediately on any suspicion of compromise.
  3. Recommended activation of the second authentication factor on the Partner Entity's accounts.
  4. Access only from devices with an up-to-date, vendor-supported operating system and browser, active antivirus software and automatic screen lock after a maximum of five minutes of inactivity. Devices on which exports or attachments are temporarily stored must have encrypted storage.
  5. Refraining from accessing the tool from public or third-party devices and from public or open wireless networks. Where there is no alternative, a mobile data network or a VPN shall be used.
  6. Logging in only through the official address of the tool, without entering credentials in links received by email or messaging, and signing out at the end of each working day.
  7. Exporting records and downloading attachments only where strictly necessary for the purposes of the Project. The files obtained shall be stored in locations with restricted access, shall be securely deleted once their purpose has been fulfilled and may not be sent by email, instant messaging or social media, or uploaded to personal cloud storage services.
  8. Deletion of the informed consent document generated as a PDF from the device on which it was downloaded, including the downloads folder and the recycle bin, once it has been attached to the corresponding record in the tool.
  9. Disclosure of the Project's Personal Data to other organizations solely through the tool's sharing function, without copying them into spreadsheets, databases or the entity's own systems or reproducing them through screenshots or photographs of the screen.
  10. Requesting Caribe Afirmativo to deactivate accounts that are no longer necessary and immediately reporting any suspicion of unauthorized access or compromised credentials, so that the affected account can be suspended as a precaution.

DAnnex D. Sub-processors

For the Processing and International Transmission activities provided for in this Agreement, the use of Sub-processors by the Partner Entity is not initially envisaged, given that the collection and recording of the Personal Data will be carried out directly by the Partner Entity, through the persons authorized for that purpose.

Should the Partner Entity subsequently need to use a Sub-processor, it must obtain the prior, express and specific authorization of Caribe Afirmativo, in accordance with clause 7 of this Agreement.

Once authorization has been given, the following information shall be incorporated into this Annex:

  1. Name or corporate name.
  2. Address.
  3. Country or jurisdiction.
  4. Name, position and contact details of the contact person.
  5. Description of the authorized Processing.

The incorporation of a Sub-processor shall not release the Partner Entity from compliance with the obligations assumed under this Agreement, nor from its liability towards Caribe Afirmativo for the Processing activities carried out by that Sub-processor.

EAnnex E. Other jurisdictions

Where the Partner Entity is located in a jurisdiction not expressly provided for in this Annex, it must verify, before beginning the International Transmission of Personal Data, whether the legislation applicable to it requires model contractual clauses other than those set out in this Agreement, or standard clauses, authorizations, additional safeguards or any other specific mechanism to lawfully carry out the transmission to Colombia. If such a requirement exists, it must inform Caribe Afirmativo and refrain from beginning the International Transmission until the corresponding instrument or safeguard has been incorporated or implemented in accordance with clause 16 of this Agreement.

Inability of the Importer to comply

The Partner Entity must inform Caribe Afirmativo if, after signing the model contractual clauses of this Agreement, it has reason to believe that it will not be able to comply with the standard contractual clauses. If the data exporter receives such a communication or otherwise discovers that the data importer can no longer comply with the standard contractual clauses, it must determine the appropriate measures to address the situation, consulting the Competent Supervisory Authority where applicable.

Such measures may consist of supplementary measures adopted by the data exporter and/or the data importer, such as administrative, physical and technical measures to guarantee security and confidentiality. The authority may also require the data exporter to suspend the transfer if it considers that there are no adequate safeguards or if the Competent Supervisory Authority so orders. This power is expressly provided for in the model contractual clauses.

E.1. Brazil

For Partner Entities located in Brazil, references in this Agreement to the Data Controller and the Data Processor shall be understood as equivalent, respectively, to the figures of Controlador and Operador provided for in Brazilian law. Accordingly, for the purposes of the International Transmission governed by this Agreement, Caribe Afirmativo shall have the status of Controlador and Data Importer and the Partner Entity that of Operador and Data Exporter.

The Processing and subsequent International Transmission of the Personal Data must be covered by the legal bases provided for in Lei nº 13.709/2018 — Lei Geral de Proteção de Dados Pessoais (LGPD). In the case of Sensitive Personal Data, the basis provided for in article 11.I of the LGPD, concerning the data subject's specific and prominent consent for specific purposes, must be observed where applicable.

The International Transmission from Brazil to Colombia must also be based on one of the mechanisms permitted by article 33 of the LGPD and by the rules issued by the Autoridade Nacional de Proteção de Dados (ANPD). Where standard contractual clauses are used as the mechanism for the International Transmission, they shall be incorporated into this Agreement in their full text and without amendment, in accordance with the requirements established by that authority.
